WHEREAS, Daniel Ramirez the owner of the above described land, has caused a

lots, blocks, easements, and rights-of-way; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the above described subdivision plat has been presented to the City of 
Cheyenne Planning Commission for consideration; the Planning Commission has recommended 
that said plat be approved by the Governing Body; and the plat has been duly executed by the 
Development Office; and 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, THAT the subdivision described as Sunnyside Addition, 13th 
Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming, be and the same hereby is approved and confirmed as presented, and 
that the Mayor and the City Clerk be and are hereby authorized, empowered, and directed to 
execute said plat, when Community Facility Fees have been paid in full.

STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
PUDC-23-145: Sunnyside Addition, 13th Filing (Expedited Review) 
REQUEST: 
A plat creating two lots. 
LOCATION: 
North of US Highway 30 and east of and adjacent to Cleveland Avenue.  
APPLICANT: 
Shane Hansen 
OWNER: 
Daniel Ramirez 
ZONING: 
MUB Mixed-use Business (Proposed to be CB Community Business).

A plat subdividing one lot into two lots for redevelopment and drainage.

PUDC-23-145  
 
 
November 6, 2023 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the Sunnyside Addition, 13th Filing Expedited Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat 
to the Governing Body. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.1.2 Preliminary Plat Approval 
• 
UDC 2.1.3 Final Plat Approval  
• 
UDC Article 4 Subdivisions 
• 
UDC Article 5 Zoning Regulations 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting approval of a plat for 2.02 acres of land. The subject property is currently vacant. 
The proposed development is for storage units on Lot 1 and a detention/drainage area on Lot 2. There is a 
zone change for this property as well, PUDC-23-120, which proposes changing the zone of the property from 
MUB to CB. The zone change was heard at the last Planning Commission meeting and was recommended for 
approval.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Preliminary Plat, the Commission 
must make the following findings required in UDC 2.1.2.d  – a detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.2.d.1) 
 
This development is in conformance with the principles and future land uses identified in 
PlanCheyenne. The property is within the Urban Service Boundary and has access to all utilities. 
The future land use map within PlanCheyenne identifies this area as developing with a mix of 
commercial uses. PlanCheyenne encourages a wide variety of land uses and activities, focused 
primarily on commercial activities, but also including other employment, public, and residential 
uses. The applicant is proposing storage units which is a commercial use. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.

PUDC-23-145  
 
 
November 6, 2023 
2) The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2) 
 
The proposed plat is a replat of a single lot. The location of open spaces, blocks, and public 
facilities are not being modified with this plat. The plat proposes designating Lot 2 as a lot for 
detention and drainage. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
3) The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.3) 
 
Community Facility Fees (4.2): 
For fees based on acreage, the area of the plat is pro-rated to the nearest tenth of an acre. 
Fees previously paid may be credited toward the payment currently due. This land was 
previously platted in 2019, and plat fees have not charged since that time.  
Total Fees due at signing of the plat: 
 
$0.00 
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure. 
Commercial Enhancement Fee $1,100.00 per acre 
 
 
Transportation Network (4.3): 
This plat is occurring within an area of the community which has been previously developed. 
The existing block and street network largely meet applicable standards.  
 
Open Space (4.4): 
Open space is required at 750sqft per unit or 8% of the gross development area, whichever 
is less. However, due to the size of the property, plat-level open space is not required. 
  
Required Improvements (4.5): 
This plat appears to be able to provide all required utilities to the lots within this 
development. 
 
Public Safety Fees (4.6): 
Public Safety Fees are required at $603.42 per 1,000 square feet of finished floor area. Per 
UDC Section 4.6.3.b, Public Safety Fees are payable at the issuance of a building permit for 
each structure.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
4) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4) 
 
No phasing is proposed with this project. 
 
Review criterion not applicable.   

PUDC-23-145  
 
 
November 6, 2023 
5) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5) 
 
Drainage: 
A Drainage Worksheet was submitted and reviewed. There were no concerns with drainage at 
the plat level for the project as presented.   
 
Traffic: 
A Traffic Worksheet was submitted and reviewed. There were no concerns with traffic at the 
plat level for the project as presented.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
  
6) Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed 
zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district, and not aggravate 
nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building and fire code access and separation 
requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6) 
 
The proposed plat proposes two lots. Lot 1 is intended to be a buildable lot while Lot 2 is 
designated as a drainage/detention area and is not a buildable lot.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the 
City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7) 
 
The plat does not propose any lots subject to this criterion. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development for 
adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan policies for that 
property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8) 
 
Properties adjacent to this plat are zoned MUB and CB. Surrounding parcels are already 
developed.    
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
9) The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 2.1.2.d.9) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed plat would not adversely affect the public health, safety, 
or general welfare. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   

PUDC-23-145  
 
 
November 6, 2023 
10) Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be developed 
or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be noted and described on 
all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 2.1.2.d.10) 
 
Remainder lots are not proposed with this plat. 
 
Review criterion not applicable. 
 
11) The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial compliance with the approved 
preliminary plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and block layout, and access, 
and any deviations in the final plat brings the application in further compliance with the 
Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.3.d.1) 
 
The Final Plat submitted with the application is substantially similar to the Preliminary Plat 
submitted. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
12) The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary plat. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.2) 
 
Through the review process and application of the Preliminary Plat, Staff does not recommend 
any conditions to the plat.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
13) The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.3) 
 
As a concurrent Preliminary and Final Plat application, this Final Plat review criterion is covered 
by the equivalent Preliminary Plat review criterion (subsection 3 above). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
1. Minor clerical corrections (corrected with revised submittal). 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff analysis is that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the expedited plat application as submitted by the applicant.  
 

PUDC-23-145  
 
 
November 6, 2023 
The area this plat is proposed in is anticipated to develop as a largely commercial or mixed use, based on 
both existing development and the patterns proposed by the comprehensive plan. The proposed plat is 
consistent with this development pattern.   
   
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the expedited preliminary plat, forwarding the Final Plat to the 
Governing Body. 
2. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat with conditions and that a final plat addressing the 
conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing Body.  
3. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant and forward the 
preliminary plat to the Governing Body. 
4. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat subject to staff’s recommended conditions and 
forward the preliminary plat to the Governing Body.  
5. Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission 
must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, 
findings for denial must be stated. 
6. Postpone the project until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add 
conditions, or decide contrary to staff’s recommendation. In the event conditions are modified, the 
conditions shall relate to applicable review criteria. In the event that the Commission acts contrary to 
staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons 
(based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the expedited Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat to the Governing 
Body. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Zoning Map  
2. Land Use Plan Map 
3. Master Application Form 
4. Preliminary Plat 
5. Final Plat

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION GRANTING AN EXCEPTION TO THE '201 
AGREEMENT' FOR THE OWNER OF THE PROPERTY LOCATED ON 
A PORTION OF THE SW1/4 SECTION 13 TO ALLOW INSTALLATION 
OF ON-SITE SEWAGE HOLDING TANKS (SEPTIC SYSTEM) LOCATED 
WITHIN LARAMIE COUNTY." 
 
WHEREAS, on April 25, 1983, the City of Cheyenne (“City”), the County of Laramie 
("County"), the City of Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities ("BOPU"), and the South Cheyenne 
Water and Sewer District (“District”) entered into an agreement entitled "Memorandum of 
Agreement: Intergovernmental Contract Agreeing to Participate in the Implementation of the 
Findings of the 201 Facilities Plan Final Report for the City of Cheyenne, the South Cheyenne 
Water and Sewer District and Laramie County"; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the April 25, 1983 agreement is commonly referred to as the "201 
Agreement"; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the 201 Agreement defines the respective responsibilities and obligations of 
the signatory governmental entities with respect to the implementation of the "201 Facilities Plan" 
that was created in response to the requirements of Section 201 of the Federal Water Pollution 
Control Act; 
 
 
WHEREAS, one of the principal purposes of the 201 Agreement is to ensure that 
wastewater (sewage) generated in the areas immediately surrounding the corporate limits of the 
City of Cheyenne would preferentially be disposed of through the sanitary sewer systems operated 
by BOPU; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the 201 Agreement defines the "service area boundary" sometimes referred 
to as the "sewerable boundary" or "201 Area" as the area that "can be provided with Municipal 
Sewage Treatment Services via gravity flow"; 
 
 
WHEREAS, Applicant is requesting a 201 Exception as owner of a commercial property 
which lies within the Laramie County jurisdiction as well as within the 201 Area, specifically 
Parcel Identification Nos. 13671330000100 which is 36.12-acres in size; legally described as 13-
67: POR OF SW1/4 SEC 13, DESC AS: BEG AT A 2 1/2" DIAMETER IRON PIPE WITH A 3" 
DIAMETER ALUMINUM CAP BEING A SEC COR COMMON TO SEC 13, 14, 23 AND 24; 
TH N 00 DEG 08' 00" E, ALONG THE SEC LINE COMMON TO SEC 13 AND 14, 1904.58' 
TO A 1/2"X24" IRON PIPE ON SD SEC LINE; TH N 00 DEG 08' 00" W, ALONG SD SEC 
LINE, 400.00'; TH N 89 DEG 56' 41" E, 367.20'; TH S 23 DEG 52' 33" E, 1516.40' TO A PNT 
ON THE R/W OF CBNQ AND SFRR; TH S 31 DEG 54' 23" W, 1081.25' TO A 3/4" IRON PIPE 
ON THE SEC LINE COMMON TO SEC 13 AND 24; TH S 89 DEG 56' 41" W, ALONG SD 
SEC LINE, 414.86' TO THE POB.; 
 
WHEREAS, the Applicant seeks to install a sewage holding tank for the purpose of 
allowing recreational vehicle holding tank dumping and said recreational vehicle holding tank 
7:34 am, Nov 07 2023

dumping into the sanitary sewer system is prohibited by the Board of Public Utilities; 
 
WHEREAS, pursuant to Section 3.4.(d) of the 201 Agreement, Laramie County 
government agreed to work with the City-County Health Unit to "ensure that no new permits for 
small wastewater treatment systems [septic systems] within the service area boundary that are 
inconsistent with the Facility Plan are issued"; 
 
WHEREAS, exceptions for the installation of small wastewater treatment systems (septic 
systems) may be granted pursuant to Section 4.1 of the 201 Agreement; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Applicants have applied for an exception pursuant to Section 4.1.(d) of 
the 201 Agreement which provides as follows:  
 
"4.1 Exceptions can be granted for the following reasons . . . (d) The exception 
demonstrates substantial compliance with the proposed Facility Plan"; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, on November 6, 2023, the City Planning Commission recommended that the 
Governing Body grant the 201 Exception based on a finding that the exception demonstrates 
substantial compliance with the proposed Facility Plan under Section 4.1.(d) of the 201 Agreement 
to permit the installation of sewage holding tanks on the subject property. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the exception is in substantial compliance with the proposed 
Facility Plan, as allowing recreational vehicle holding tank dumping directly into the sanitary 
sewer is more detrimental to the public health, safety, and welfare than dumping of such 
recreational vehicle holding tank contents into a self-contained sewage holding tank which is 
regularly pumped; and 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the City of Cheyenne hereby consents to the granting 
of an exception pursuant to Section 4.1.(d) of the 201 Agreement to permit installation of a septic 
holding system, subject to the following conditions: (1) The sewage holding tank(s) shall be 
serviced in a manner as required by Cheyenne-Laramie County Health Department; (2); The 
facility shall only be used for the dumping of recreational vehicles into the holding tanks; and (3) 
The future office building will need to be reevaluated at the time of proposed installation.

STAFF REPORT  
 
 
FILE: 
PUDC-23-165: RV Dump Station Holding Tank - 201 Exception  
REQUEST: 
201 Exception to allow installation of a self-contained sanitary septic system for a 
recreational vehicle dump station.  
LOCATION: 
Portion of SW ¼ Section 13, located east of I-25 and South of W College Drive. 
APPLICANT: 
Scott Larson, Benchmark Engineers 
OWNER: 
Thomas Haynes

CASE PLANNER: 
Connor White, Planner II

2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
Subject 
Property 

PUDC-23-165 
 
 
November 6, 2023 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION(s): 
 
Option 1: 
Move to recommend the City Council approve the exception to the 201 Facilities Plan requirement for 
City sewer service for a recreational vehicle dump station and permit installation of septic holding 
tank(s), subject to Staff’s recommended conditions.  
 
Option 2:  
Move to recommend the City Council approve the exception to the 201 Facilities Plan requirement for 
City sewer service for a recreational vehicle dump station and permit installation of septic holding 
tank(s), and allow the connection of a future office building into the holding tank, subject to Staff’s 
recommended conditions.  
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
Cheyenne Municipal Code Chapter 1.16 (Annexation and Infrastructure Improvements)  
• 
Cheyenne Municipal Code Chapter 13.12 (Sewer System) 
• 
201 Intergovernmental Agreement to participate in the implementation of the 201 Facilities 
Plan Final Report for the City of Cheyenne, the South Cheyenne Water and Sewer District, and 
Laramie County (1983) (IGA) 
• 
201 Facilities Plan Final Report (1982) 
• 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
The request for an exception must be submitted to the Planning Commission for review.  After 
Commission review, the exception would be approved or denied by the City Council. If the property 
seeking the exception is outside the Corporate Limits of the City and inside the 201 Service Area 
Boundary, then County Commissioner approval is also required. 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
In 1982 the City, County, and south Cheyenne Water and Sewer District executed an intergovernmental 
agreement to implement the 1982 Wastewater Facilities Master Plan. The plan and the IGA were 
intended to satisfy the Federal Clean Water Act requirements and to protect the safety and quality of 
the region’s groundwater from pollution, specifically from septic system failures.   
 
The IGA requires connection to sewer systems for development of properties lying within the Urban 
Service Area identified in the Plan. The Facilities Plan defines the Urban Service Area as the area where 
“Municipal Sewerage Treatment services can be provided via gravity flow” [Section 1.0, Definitions of 
the IGA]. The IGA further states that the City of Cheyenne is to limit the use of on-site wastewater 
treatment systems within the Urban Service Area; this would include septic systems and package 
wastewater treatment plants. The IGA states that the City and County will not approve any development 
which is inconsistent with the Facility Plan unless granted an exception pursuant to Sections 4.0 to 4.7 of 
the IGA. 
 
The applicant applied for a permit to allow a self-contained septic holding tank. The permit was denied. 
After denial, the applicant was eligible to apply for an exception pursuant to the IGA. The request for an 
exception must be submitted to the Planning Commission for review. After Commission review, the 
exception would be approved or denied by the City Council and the County Commissioners if the 
property seeking the exception is outside the Corporate Limits of the City and inside the 201 Service 
Area Boundary. 

PUDC-23-165 
 
 
November 6, 2023 
 
The City of Cheyenne Annexation Policies state that for land that is annexed, the owner must provide 
off-site infrastructure which shall include water lines and sanitary sewer lines [Section 1.16.040 
Ordinance 3723, dated August 28, 2006]. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING:

ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to approve the exception, the Commission must make the 
following findings per Sections 4.0 to 4.7 of the 201 IGA. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
Section 4.0: EXCEPTIONS FOR DEVELOPMENT THAT IS NOT CONSISTENT WITH THE PROPOSED FACILITY 
PLAN: 
 
4.1 Exceptions can be granted for the following reasons: 
(a) The proposed exception conforms with the criteria for exceptions in The City of Cheyenne 
Annexation Policies. 
(b) The exception can be shown not to be detrimental to the public health, safety and welefare [sic]. 
(c) The exception will alleviate a clearly demonstrable hardship and compliance with the Facility Plan 
will exacerbate this hardship. A hardship will be determined based upon a cost/effectiveness study of 
sewer service provision to the property seeking the exception. 
(d) The exception demonstrates substantial compliance with the proposed Facility Plan. 
 
The wording of the IGA does not appear to require all 4 reasons to be met (no “and” is present). This 
proposal does meet all of these reasons.  
 
Reason (a): This property is outside of the City limits and does not have sanitary sewer readily 
available. Off-site sanitary sewer main extension would be required. The closest sewer main is in W 
College Drive at the intersection of Southwest Drive. This proposal seeks an exception from tying 
into the sanitary sewer system for a future office building and to allow an RV dump station. The 
Board of Public Utilities prohibits recreational vehicle dump stations from connecting to the sanitary 
sewer lines.   
 
Reason (b): The facility proposed does not have a leach field and does not discharge into the 
surrounding environment. Due to the nature of recreational vehicle waste, connecting a recreational 
vehicle dump station directly into the sanitary sewer system would increase the probability of 
wastes prohibited by City Code 13.12.195 entering the sanitary sewer system, and therefore it may 
be considered more detrimental to the public health, safety, and welfare to have a dump station tie 

PUDC-23-165 
 
 
November 6, 2023 
 
into the sewer system then it would be to have the dump station be self-contained. The proposed 
future office building would also connect into the holding tank and the applicant has indicated that 
it would be pumped more regularly at that time. By pumping the holding tank more regularly it 
would make the holding tank even less detrimental to the public health, safety, and welfare. 
 
Reason (c): The property does not have sanitary sewer readily available. The closest sewer main is 
within College Drive to the north. The applicant has provided a cost estimate to extend the sewer 
main compared to installing the proposed holding tank / dump station. The estimated cost to tie 
into the City sanitary sewer system would be approximately $948,750.00. The cost to install the 
proposed dump station would cost approximately $9,300.00. Extending the sewer main and tying 
into the City sewer system would be a hardship based upon a cost/effectiveness study.  
 
Reason (d): The facility would maintain the principal goal of the IGA – that is to prevent the 
environmental pollution of the land. The facilities proposed would provide a service which may 
assist in preventing illegal recreational vehicle sewage dumping in other locations in the community. 
The system proposed is self-contained and would require pumping of the holding tank. Should the 
office building be added later and connected into the holding tank, the applicant has indicated that 
the tank would then be pumped more often.   
 
4.2. Exceptions may only be considered if the proposal has been denied due to non-conformance with 
the proposed Facility Plan. 
 
The applicant applied for a permit to allow a self-contained septic holding tank on September 27, 
2023.  The permit was denied on October 18, 2023. After denial, the applicant was eligible to apply 
for an exception pursuant to the IGA.  The exception request was filed with the Cheyenne-Laramie 
County Health Department on October 23, 2023 and forwarded to the City for review and 
consideration on October 23, 2023.  
 
4.3. The request for exception must be submitted to the Planning Commission for review. 
 
This proposal is being submitted to the Planning Commission. 
 
4.4. After Planning Commission review, the exception will be approved or disapproved by: 
(a) The City Council if the property seeking exception is within the Corporate Limits; (b) The City 
Council and County Commissioners if the property seeking exception is outside the Corporate Limits 
and inside the Service Area Boundary. 
 
The property seeking an exception is outside the City limits of the City of Cheyenne, and the 
exception will be submitted to the City Council and County Commissioners after the Planning 
Commission review. 
 
4.5 (Due to a formatting error in the original Agreement, there is no Section 4.5) 
 
4.6. The replacement of an existing on-site wastewater treatment system on a property that is neither 
contiguous with the Cheyenne Corporate Limits nor within the DISTRICT's boundaries will not require 
an exception under this Agreement. 
 
This Section is not applicable to this proposal. 
 

PUDC-23-165 
 
 
November 6, 2023 
 
4.7. The installation of a new on-site wastewater system on a single lot within the Service Area 
Boundary that is not contiguous with the Cheyenne Corporate Limits nor within the DISTRICT's 
boundaries will not require an exception under this Agreement, provided that the lot meets all the 
requirements of the Subdivision Regulations and any other requirements of this Agreement and was 
platted prior to the effective date of this Agreement. 
 
This Section is not applicable to this proposal. 
 
RELEVANT PLANCHEYENNE POLICIES  
The following PlanCheyenne polices are related to this project: 
 
Foundation 1—Growing as a Community of Choice 
Policy 1.1.C: Regional Coordination on Land Use and Development Issues. 
 
Foundation 3—Fostering a Vital Economy and Activity Centers 
Policy 3.1.C: Business-Supportive Environment 
 
Foundation 7—Developing in a Fiscally Responsible Way 
Principle 7.1: Our community will coordinate and plan for growth regionally to ensure efficient 
and cost-effective services and utilities. 
Principle 7.2: The Cheyenne Area will provide adequate public facilities and services for current 
and future residents in a fiscally responsible manner. 
 
The IGA was created to coordinate regionally on environmental health. The proposed holding tanks 
provide a service commonly available with recreational vehicle type businesses. The proposed dump 
station is also a service which may be beneficial to the public health, safety, and welfare. The exception 
request complies with the goals of PlanCheyenne.    
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
As noted above in staff evaluation of the review criteria, due to the nature of the service provided by a 
recreational vehicle dumping station, Staff believes that the application meets the requirements to qualify 
for a “substantial compliance” 201 Exception. 
 
Staff believes that the proposed future office buildings connection into the holding tank also meets the 
requirements to qualify for a “substantial compliance” 201 Exception. It can be reevaluated at a later date 
and not approved at this time. Staff believes that if sewer becomes more readily available that the office 
building should be connected into the city sanitary sewer system by way of annexation or by seeking an 
outside user’s agreement.  
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend the Governing Body approve the exception as submitted by the applicant. 
2. Recommend the Governing Body approve the exception subject to Staff’s recommended 
conditions (Staff Recommendation). 
3. Recommend denial of  the exception based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission must 
conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, findings 
for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the item until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 

PUDC-23-165 
 
 
November 6, 2023 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add 
conditions, or decide contrary to Staff’s recommendation. In the event conditions are modified, the 
conditions shall relate to applicable review criteria. In the event that the Planning Commission acts 
contrary to staff’s recommendation, the Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review 
criteria) for a recommendation to approve or deny.   
 
STAFF’S RECOMMENDATION(s): 
 
Option 1: 
Staff recommends approval of the exception for a portion of SW ¼ Section 13, located east of I-25 and 
South of W College Drive to allow a septic holding tank subject to the following conditions: 
1. The sewage holding tank(s) shall be serviced in a manner as required by Cheyenne-Laramie 
County Health Department. 
2. The facility shall only be used for the dumping of recreational vehicles into the holding tanks. 
3. The future office building will need to be reevaluated at the time of proposed installation.  
 
Option 2:  
Staff recommends approval of the exception for a portion of SW ¼ Section 13, located east of I-25 and 
South of W College Drive to allow a septic holding tank and the future connection of an office building 
into the proposed holding tank subject to the following conditions: 
1. The sewage holding tank(s) shall be serviced in a manner as required by Cheyenne-Laramie 
County Health Department. 
2. Should city sanitary sewer become more readily available the proposed future office building 
will be required to connect into the city sanitary sewer system by way of annexation or by 
seeking an outside user’s agreement.  
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
 
1. Applicant Exception Request Letter 
2. Cheyenne-Laramie County Health Denial Letter 
3. Submitted Cheyenne-Laramie County Health Application  

201 Exception Appeal Request, I25 RV Storage Facility (SW1/4, S.13, T13, R67)                           Page 1 of 2 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
October 23, 2023 
 
Mr. Charles Bloom 
City of Cheyenne Development Director 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Room 202 
Cheyenne, WY 82001 
 
RE: 
201 Exception Appeal Request 
 
I25 RV Storage Facility (SW1/4, S.13, T13, R67) 
 
Dear Mr. Bloom: 
 
We hereby request an exception/waiver of the 201 requirements for tying into City sewer for the above 
referenced facility in order to appeal the decision by the Health Department. The small wastewater 
application for an RV dump station holding tank (no leach field) submitted to the Health Department was 
denied due to the property being within the 201 area/boundary.  
 
The following is our justification for the request: 
 
1. The BOPU generally does not allow RV dump stations to use the public sanitary sewer system. 
2. The 201 Agreement defines the “Service Area Boundary” as “A Boundary that defines areas that: 1. 
Can be provided with Municipal Sewage Treatment Services via gravity flow,”. This property is unable 
to tie into any existing City sanitary sewer system (i.e., in West College/Southwest Drive or in 
Granite Peak Drive) via gravity flow and would require a lift station to tie in. 
3. The cost to tie into the existing City sanitary sewer system would be approximately $948,750 
which is prohibitive. The cost to install the proposed RV dump station holding tank is 
approximately $9,300, which is significantly lower, realistic, and attainable than tying into the City 
sewer system. 
4. This request is not detrimental to the public health, safety, and welfare because the proposed plan 
will not discharge any sewage into the ground, etc. via a leach field. It will be held in a storage 
tank, pumped out, and taken to an appropriate and legal location to dispose of the sewage. 
Therefore, there is no sewage that will be discharged into the ground that could potentially impact 
the groundwater, etc. Alarms will be installed to ensure the tank does not overfill.  
5. The developer is not opposed to ultimately tying into City water and/or sewer when and if it 
becomes more readily available and the cost to tie in is not prohibitive. Even then, the RV dump 
station will most likely not be allowed to tie into the City sewer unless BOPU changes their 
position and will accept the sewage from an RV dump station.  
6. The current plan is to not have an office with staff and restrooms. If that changes with future 
phases of the project, it will be tied into the holding tank and the pumping of the holding tank 
would occur more frequently. 

201 Exception Appeal Request, I25 RV Storage Facility (SW1/4, S.13, T13, R67)                           Page 2 of 2 
 
7. Full design plans for the RV dump holding tank will be provided to the Health Department for 
approval if this exception/waiver is granted. 
8. Bish’s RV, which is located directly west and across I25 of this property was granted an exception 
2 years ago for the exact same situation – a holding tank for an RV dump station. 
 
Based on the above information, we believe this request is reasonable and justified. Please let me know if 
you have any questions or require additional information.  
 
Sincerely, 
 
 
 
Scott D. Larson, P.E. 
President 
 
cc:  Mr. Justin Arnold, Laramie County Planning Department 

 
Certified 
 
October 18, 2023 
 
Thomas E. Haynes 
6304 River Run Dr 
Sebastian, FL 32958 
 
Dear Mr. Haynes, 
 
On September 27, 2023, our office received your application for a permit to 
install a small wastewater holding tank on a parcel of property located in 
portion of SW ¼, Section 13, Township 13, Range 67.  After careful review of 
your application this office cannot issue a small wastewater permit at this 
time for the following reasons: 
 
1. 
Installation of a new septic holding tank for a new business on a 
property which is contiguous to the City of Cheyenne is prohibited in 
the Service Area boundary and is not in compliance with the “201 
Facilities Plan” or the “201” agreement.   
 
In order to appeal the Cheyenne Laramie County Public Health final decision; 
you must file a written request for hearing with the Laramie County 
Commissioners within sixty (60) days of receipt of this denial notification.  
Paperwork must be filed with the Cheyenne and Laramie County Planning 
Commissions, County Commissioners and City Council and request made to 
be placed on their agenda.  At those meetings, you must demonstrate to the 
governing bodies that your request for exception should be granted by 
meeting the criteria for exception. Your application/inspection fee for the 
small wastewater system will be held until we hear from you. 
 
Cheyenne Laramie County Public Health may be able to assist you in getting 
on the agendas and filing of paperwork to the governing bodies. A consulting 
fee may be applicable. This assistance is in no way meant to be indicative of 
support for your request.  
 
If you have any questions concerning anything in this letter, please feel free 
to contact me at 100 Central Ave or call at 633-4090. 

 
 
Sincerely, 
 
 
 
 
Jennifer Escobedo, Director, Division of Environmental Health

A PORTION OF THE SW1/4 SECTION 13 TO ALLOW INSTALLATION 
OF ON-SITE SEWAGE HOLDING TANKS (SEPTIC SYSTEM) LOCATED 
WITHIN LARAMIE COUNTY." 
 
WHEREAS, on April 25, 1983, the City of Cheyenne ("City"), the County of Laramie 
(“County”), the City of Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities ("BOPU"), and the South Cheyenne 
Water and Sewer District ("District") entered into an agreement entitled "Memorandum of 
Agreement: Intergovernmental Contract Agreeing to Participate in the Implementation of the 
Findings of the 201 Facilities Plan Final Report for the City of Cheyenne, the South Cheyenne 
Water and Sewer District and Laramie County" (f/k/a "201 Agreement"); 
 
 
WHEREAS, the 201 Agreement defines the respective responsibilities and obligations of 
the signatory governmental entities with respect to the implementation of the "201 Facilities Plan" 
that was created in response to the requirements of Section 201 of the Federal Water Pollution 
Control Act; 
 
 
WHEREAS, one of the principal purposes of the 201 Agreement is to ensure that 
wastewater (i.e. sewage) generated in the areas immediately surrounding the corporate limits of 
the City of Cheyenne would preferentially be disposed of through the sanitary sewer systems 
operated by BOPU; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the 201 Agreement defines the "service area boundary" sometimes referred 
to as the "sewerable boundary" or "201 Area" as the area that "can be provided with Municipal 
Sewage Treatment Services via gravity flow"; 
 
 
WHEREAS, an Applicant is requesting a 201 Exception as owner of a commercial property 
which lies within the Laramie County jurisdiction as well as within the 201 Area, specifically

ON THE SEC LINE COMMON TO SEC 13 AND 24; TH S 89 DEG 56' 41" W, ALONG SD 
SEC LINE, 414.86' TO THE POB.; 
 
WHEREAS, the Applicant seeks to install as septic holding system at a cost of 
approximately nine thousand three hundred dollars ($9,300) rather than pay an estimated nine 
hundred forty-eight thousand seven hundred fifty thousand dollars ($948,750) cost to extend 
sanitary sewer service from the intersection of College Drive and Southwest Drive;  
 
12:57 pm, Nov 20 2023

WHEREAS, pursuant to Section 3.4.(d) of the 201 Agreement, Laramie County 
government agreed to work with the City-County Health Unit to "ensure that no new permits for 
small wastewater treatment systems [septic systems] within the service area boundary that are 
inconsistent with the Facility Plan are issued"; 
 
WHEREAS, exceptions for the installation of small wastewater treatment systems (septic 
systems) may be granted pursuant to Section 4.1 of the 201 Agreement; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Applicants have applied for an exception pursuant to Section 4.1.(c) of 
the 201 Agreement which provides as follows:  
 
4.1  Exceptions can be granted for the following reasons . . . (c) The exception will alleviate 
a clearly demonstrable hardship and compliance with the Facility Plan will exacerbate this 
hardship. A hardship will be determined based upon a cost/effectiveness study of sewer 
service provision to the property seeking the exception; 
 
 
WHEREAS, on November 6, 2023, the City Planning Commission recommended that the 
Governing Body grant the 201 Exception based on a finding that the exception demonstrates 
substantial compliance with the proposed Facility Plan under Section 4.1.(c) of the 201 Agreement 
to permit the installation of sewage holding tanks on the subject property; and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the exception is in substantial compliance with the proposed 
Facility Plan, as the property does not have sanitary sewer readily available. The closest sewer 
main is within College Drive to the north. The applicant has provided a cost estimate to extend the 
sewer main compared to installing the proposed holding tank / dump station. The estimated cost 
to tie into the City sanitary sewer system would be approximately $948,750.00. The cost to install 
the proposed dump station would cost approximately $9,300.00. Extending the sewer main and 
tying into the City sewer system would be a hardship based upon a cost/effectiveness study; 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the City of Cheyenne hereby consents to the granting 
of an exception pursuant to Section 4.1.(c) of the 201 Agreement to permit installation of a septic 
holding system, subject to the following conditions: 
 
1. 
The sewage holding tank(s) shall be serviced in a manner as required by Cheyenne-
Laramie County Health Department. 
 
2. 
Should city sanitary sewer become more readily available (within 1000-feet of the 
property if no lift station is required or within 100-feet of the property) the proposed 
future office building will be required to connect into the city sanitary sewer system 
by way of annexation or by seeking an outside user’s agreement.

2023.

MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Jeanie Vetter, PLA, Parks and Greenway Planner  
Subject: A RESOLUTION SUPPORTING THE CONVERSION OF A PORTION OF DUTCHER BALL 
FIELDS, LOCATED AT 4200 CONVERSE AVENUE, CHEYENNE WYOMING FROM 
PERPETUAL PUBLIC OUTDOOR RECREATION USE AND COMMITING TO ENCUMBER 
THE ROTARY PARK PROPERTY TO BE DONATED BY THE CHEYENNE ROTARY CLUB 
FOUNDATION TO INCLUDE A DEED RESTRICTION FOR PERPETUAL PUBLIC OUTDOOR 
RECREATION USE PURSUANT TO THE REQUIREMENTS SET FORTH IN THE LAND AND 
WATER CONSERVATION FUND ACT. 
 
Date: 
November 21, 2023 
 
Background:  
 
The item for consideration is a resolution of support required by the LWCF conversion process to request 
conversion of a portion of Dutcher Ball Fields, approximately 2.5 acres, to be removed from perpetual 
public outdoor recreation use for the new city fire station.  The LWCF regulations require that we provide 
other suitable properties that meet LWCF requirements such as being of at least equal fair market value 
and of reasonably equivalent usefulness as trade.  The city has already agreed to restrict the Keizer, Lewark 
and Winkler properties for this purpose but, unfortunately, we have decided that we need further 
environmental investigations on the Winkler property due to its previous use as an auto salvage yard.     A 
federal standards appraisal and review have been completed for the Rotary Park property and it shows, 
combined with the appraised value of both the Lewark and Keizer purchases, an equal and/or greater fair 
market value.  The Rotary Park property will provide land for park amenities as well as improvements to 
the Dry Creek corridor for water quality, storage, and public enjoyment.

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "RESOLUTION SUPPORTING THE CONVERSION OF A PORTION OF 
DUTCHER BALL FIELDS, LOCATED AT 4200 CONVERSE AVENUE, 
CHEYENNE WYOMING FROM PERPETUAL PUBLIC OUTDOOR 
RECREATION USE AND COMMITING TO ENCUMBER THE ROTARY

CONSERVATION FUND ACT OF 1965." 
 
WHEREAS, the City desires to convert approximately two point five (2.5) acres of the 
Dutcher Ball Field property to a non-recreational use to build a municipal fire station; 
 
WHEREAS, the Land and Water Conservation Fund (LWCF) Act of 1965 was enacted to 
help preserve, develop, and ensure access to outdoor recreation facilities to strengthen the health 
of U.S. citizens. The law created the LWCF in the U.S. Treasury as a funding source to implement 
its outdoor recreation goals. Parks or recreation areas acquired or developed with LWCF assistance 
(in whole or part) are now referred to as "LWCF-assisted areas" and/or "LWCF boundary areas."; 
 
WHEREAS, the LWCF requires that no property acquired or developed with LWCF 
assistance areas shall be converted to other than public outdoor recreation uses without the approval 
of the Secretary of the Interior. If the Secretary finds the property to be in accordance with the then 
existing SCORP and only upon such conditions as the Secretary deems necessary to assure the 
substitution of other recreation properties of at least equal fair market value and of reasonably 
equivalent usefulness and location; 
 
WHEREAS, the City intends to take actions to accept donation of the twelve point six 
(12.06) acre Rotary Park property and agrees to allow deed restriction for perpetual outdoor 
recreation use as described in attached exhibits, at the time of purchase; 
 
WHEREAS, the description of the twelve point six (12.06) acres of the proposed 
replacement property, Rotary Park, is described as follows:

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-11-07_TotalExtraction_31747160.pdf
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
City of Cheyenne Council Members 
FROM: 
Robin Lockman, City Treasurer 
DATE: 
November 21, 2023 
SUBJECT: 
Cost-of-living wage increase to be considered for approval 
On November 27, 2023, the Governing Body will be referring the attached resolution to the Finance 
Committee to authorize a cost-of-living salary increase for all permanent full and part-time employees of 
the City of Cheyenne.  
The latest cost-of-living index for southeast Wyoming is 4.3 percent year-over-year for the second quarter 
of 2023.  Considering this, city administration is proposing that all permanent full and part-time employees, 
excluding elected officials and employees of the Board of Public Utilities, receive a 3 percent cost-of-living 
salary adjustment effective January 1, 2023.   
Mayor Collins, facing the challenging task of balancing the City’s long-term financial stability with 
the importance of providing a cost-of-living wage adjustment to the City’s valued workforce, has 
decided to use a somewhat more conservative approach in this request compared with the cost-of-living 
index.  This is due to leading economic indicators continuing to signal an impending recession in the 
United States next year.  Mayor Collins is attempting to strike a delicate equilibrium between fiscal 
prudence and maintaining a supportive work environment during these uncertain economic times. 
The estimated six-month cost for this request is $526,094.31 from General Fund reserves and $167,409.83 
for all other City funds.  A budget amendment will be completed in December to appropriate funds for 
these increases.  
As always, if you have any questions, please give me a call at 637-6336 or email at 
rlockman@cheyennecity.org.  

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING A THREE (3) PERCENT COST-OF-
LIVING SALARY ADJUSTMENT FOR ALL PERMANENT FULL AND 
PART-TIME EMPLOYEES OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE." 
WHEREAS, the City recognizes the rising cost-of-living affecting its employees; 
WHEREAS, maintaining a competitive and fair compensation structure is essential to 
attract and retain skilled and dedicated personnel; 
WHEREAS, a cost-of-living increase is an effective way to address financial challenges 
faced by employees;  
WHEREAS, on October 31, 2023 the State of Wyoming, Economic Analysis Division, 
released the Wyoming Cost-of-Living Index for the second quarter of 2023, which measures 
Wyoming inflation rates compared to one year ago;  
WHEREAS, the regional inflation rate for Southeast Wyoming, which covers Albany, 
Carbon, Goshen, Laramie, Niobrara and Platte Counties, is 4.3 percent; and 
NOW THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, effective January 1, 2024, that all eligible 
permanent full and part-time City employees, excluding elected officials and employees of the 
City of Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities, will receive a three (3) percent cost-of-living salary 
increase, 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that employees with salaries exceeding their position's 
pay grade in the City's compensation plan as a result of this cost-of-living adjustment will instead 
receive a pro-rata one-time payment in lieu of a salary increase in the amounts approved for their 
position, 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the City Treasurer shall create a budget 
reappropriation to allocate the necessary funds from reserves and grant funding sources to 
accommodate this cost-of-living adjustment. 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _________________, 2023. 
__________________________________________ 
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
(SEAL) 
ATTEST: 
____________________________________ 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
8:43 am, Nov 20 2023


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-11-07_TotalExtraction_25167355.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION PERMITTING THE POSSESSION OF OPEN 
CONTAINERS WITHIN THE DOWNTOWN DISTRICT BOUNDARIES 
FOR NEW YEARS EVE ON DECEMBER 31, 2023." 
 
 
WHEREAS, Section 5.12.110(G)(3) of the Municipal Code permits the possession of open 
containers of wine and malt beverages within the boundaries of the downtown development district 
between the hours of 12:00 p.m. and 10:00 p.m. year round;  
 
WHEREAS, under Section 5.12.110(G)(3)(d), the governing body may modify by 
resolution the restrictions imposed on the periods of time and the boundaries of special areas or 
districts, beverage distribution, tracking, or event control;  
 
WHEREAS, the city recognizes the value of community engagement and desires to further 
enhance the celebration of New Year's Eve for all residents and visitors, including those who may 
prefer not to consume alcoholic beverages; 
 
WHEREAS, there is a desire to promote inclusivity by allowing open containers during 
the New Year’s Eve Ball Drop event, where alcohol is not being served, to encourage community 
members to actively participate in the festivities; 
 
WHEREAS, Visit Cheyenne requests the possession of open containers of wine and malt 
beverages within the boundaries of the downtown development district be permitted for the 
purpose of New Year’s Eve on Saturday, December 31, 2023, from 10:00 p.m. until January 1, 
2024, at 2:00 a.rn.; 
 
WHEREAS, the event organizers will comply with all laws, rules, and regulations to ensure 
the safety of the community; and 
 
WHEREAS, adherence to the following requirements shall be required for participation by 
alcohol vendors and consumers when open containers are permitted as designated herein: 
 
1. The only containers authorized for alcoholic beverages in the special district 
downtown area’s public spaces, for the day specified herein, shall be plastic cups. Glass 
containers are strictly prohibited; 
 
2. No outside alcoholic beverage, wine or malt liquor may be brought into or 
consumed within the open container area and any violation of this provision shall constitute 
a violation of the City’s open container prohibition and may incur additional civil or 
criminal liability; and 
 
3. Nothing herein shall be interpreted to authorize the possession of open containers 
of alcoholic liquor or malt beverages in or on motor vehicles.  
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, the possession of open containers of wine and malt beverages 
within the boundaries of the downtown development district shall be permitted for New Year’s 
Eve, during the dates and times designated herein. 
 
9:26 am, Nov 21 2023

2023. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
 
(SEAL)

Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

MEMORANDUM 
 
DATE: 
October 18, 2023 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick J. Collins 
 
 
Members of the City Council 
 
 
FROM: 
Thomas D. Cobb, PE, City Engineer 
 
 
SUBJECT: 
Drainage Easement granted by WMC, Cheyenne, LLC to the City of 
Cheyenne, a Wyoming municipal corporation 
 
 
Please find the attached the proposed drainage easement for your acceptance.   
 
Reason for Easement:  
 
The primary reason for the Easement is to ensure on behalf of the public that perpetual 
mainteance is provided in a private drainage facility to remain in “Good Working Order.” 
 
 “Good working order” is defined as the drainage system operating as designed and constructed 
in accordance with the as-built construction drawings on file with the City including, but not 
limited to, ensuring that the structures and Easement Area land surface are free of debris, 
impoundments, and/or alterations to the drainage flow.  
 
 
Cost and Funding: 
 
No impacts are anticipated at this time.  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
A COMMUNITY OF CHOICE 
OFFICE OF THE CITY ENGINEER 
2101 O’Neil Avenue Room 206  
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 
Phone (307)637-6268    Fax (307)637-6256 

Page 1 of 3 
DRAINAGE EASEMENT AGREEMENT 
 
KNOW ALL MEN BY THESE PRESENTS that, WMC Cheyenne, LLC (“Grantor”), 
whose address is 4245 East 46th Avenue Denver CO, 80216, in consideration of the sum of Ten 
Dollars ($10.00) and other good and valuable consideration, receipt and sufficiency of which is 
hereby acknowledged by and between the parties, does hereby grant to the City of Cheyenne, a 
Wyoming municipal corporation (“City”), whose address is 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming 82001, and to its successors and assigns, a non-exclusive drainage easement, legally 
described on Exhibit A, attached hereto and incorporated herein. 
 
RECITALS 
 
WHEREAS, the City desires to obtain a drainage easement for the purposes of providing, 
ensuring the maintenance of a storm water conveyance/ drainage facility on and through the 
property more particularly described and depicted in Exhibit A (“Easement Area”); 
 
WHEREAS, Grantor acknowledges that they have the obligation to maintain the drainage 
facilities within the easement area in accordance with this agreement; 
 
WHEREAS, Grantor is willing to grant such easement to Grantee on and subject to the 
terms and conditions of this agreement; and 
 
WHEREAS, Grantor states it is the lawful owner of the real property described herein, that 
it has good and lawful right and authority to grant, sell, and convey said property, or any part 
thereof, and that it warrants the title of said property. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the foregoing premises and the mutual agreement 
of the parties hereto, the parties hereby agree as follows: 
 
1. 
Drainage Easement.  Grantor executes this easement in favor of City, its 
successors and assigns, the right and privilege to ensure on behalf of the public that the facility 
remains in Good Working Order.  Such grant includes all rights and privileges necessary or 
convenient for the full enjoyment and use thereof for the above-mentioned purposes including, but 
not limited to, the right of ingress and egress over Grantor’s adjoining property in order to exercise 
the explicit right to remedy any drainage issues on the property. 
 
2. 
Modification.  The Grantor shall be prohibited from altering or placing anything 
in the Easement Area which shall obstruct or impede the flow of storm water or surface drainage 
from the Grantor’s property. Grantor agrees not to build or to convey to others permission to build 
any structures or improvements on, over, across, in, through, or under the above-described 
Easement Area, or to develop or allow the development of the Easement Area in any manner which 
obstructs or interferes with the function or capacity of the drainage facilities. However, if approved 
in writing by the City, the Grantor may construct and/or install surface improvements to its 
property, including paved driveways, parking and/or walkways, landscaping, utilities and/or 
similar improvements, which improvements do not then or later interfere with the aforementioned 
purposes of the Easement Area. 
11:09 am, Oct 18 2023

Page 2 of 3 
3. 
Maintenance.  Grantor shall maintain the drainage facilities contained within the 
Easement Area in good working order.  “Good working order” is defined as the drainage system 
operating as designed and constructed in accordance with the as-built construction drawings on 
file with the City including, but not limited to, ensuring that the structures and Easement Area land 
surface are free of debris, impoundments, and/or alterations to the drainage flow. 
 
4. 
Shared Use.  The Grantor reserves the right to make any use of the Easement Area 
which is not inconsistent with the rights herein conveyed to the City, and which does not interfere 
with the aforementioned purposes of the Easement Area. 
 
It is understood and agreed that the enforcement and conditions of this agreement, and all 
rights contained herein, shall be strictly reserved to the Grantor and the City.  Nothing contained 
in this agreement shall give any claim or right whatsoever by any third person or entity.  It is the 
express intention of the Grantor and the City that any such person or entity, other than the Grantor 
or the City, that receives a service or benefit under this agreement shall be deemed an incidental 
third-party beneficiary only. 
 
This grant of easement is binding upon the heirs, executors, administrators, successors, and 
assigns of the parties hereto.

MEMO 
 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick J. Collins, City of Cheyenne 
Members of the City Council, City of Cheyenne 
 
FROM: 
Wesley D. Bay, P.E., Deputy City Engineer 
 
DATE: 
November 6, 2023 
 
SUBJECT: 
Professional Service Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Ardurra for 
the Charles Street Drainage and Roadway Improvements Design Project.  
 
 
Please find the attached Professional Service Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and 
Ardurra for engineering services related to the Charles Street Drainage and Roadway 
Improvements Design Project. The design will provide roadway design from College Drive to the 
US 30 Service Road. Storm sewer for Charles Street, Monroe Avenue, and Cheyenne Street are 
included in the design tasks. Figure 1 shown below shows the limits of the project. 
 
 
  
 Engineering Staff evaluates fee proposals for engineering services based on time and material 
estimates for the specific project.  Those costs are readily tracked and verified during design 
operations and the City only pays for the actual time expended. We believe this agreement will 
provide the required services to complete the design portion of the project. 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
CITY ENGINEER’S OFFICE 
2101 O’Neil Avenue  
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 

 
The fees include services for project management, topographical surveying, geotechnical 
investigation, utility investigation, roadway and storm sewer design, and plan creation. Funding 
for this project will come from the 2019 1% Optional Sales Tax Fund (5th Penny). The total 
professional services will be a cost not-to-exceed $152,200. We respectfully request your approval 
of this professional services agreement.

PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
for

This Professional Services Agreement (“Agreement”) is made and entered into by and 
between the City of Cheyenne, located at 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 
(“City”), and Ardurra, located at 205 Storey Blvd., Suite 120 (“Consultant”).  This Agreement 
shall become effective upon the date of the last signature affixed hereto. 
 
RECITALS 
 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by 
and between the parties as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF WORK 
The Consultant agrees to provide services as described for the above-mentioned project, 
in accordance with the Scope of Work attached hereto as Exhibit A, incorporated by 
reference and expressly made a part hereof. 
 
2. 
TIME OF PERFORMANCE 
This Agreement shall commence on the date the last required signature is attached hereto 
and shall continue in full force and effect until the scope of work is completed in 
accordance with Exhibit A, unless this Agreement is earlier terminated pursuant to its 
provisions. 
 
3. 
COMPENSATION 
In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the 
Consultant will bill the City and the City agrees to pay Consultant a not to exceed amount 
of One-Hundred Fifty-Two Thousand Two-Hundred Dollars ($152,200) as stated in 
Exhibit A.  
 
4. 
CONSULTANT RESPONSIBILITY 
In providing services under this Agreement, the Consultant shall perform in a manner 
consistent with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances. Upon notice to the 
Consultant, the Consultant will, without additional compensation, correct those services 
not meeting such a standard. 
 
5. 
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS 
The Parties agree that they will perform their obligations as provided in this Agreement 
in accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances.

6. 
DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE 
In compliance with the Drug-Free Workplace Act of November 1988, the City of 
Cheyenne has established an Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy that pertains to 
alcohol and drug usage by City Employees. All architects, engineers, and other 
consultants under contract with City of Cheyenne, and their employees and 
sub-consultants, are required to comply with the provisions of the City’s Alcohol and 
Controlled Substance Policy for drug and/or alcohol usage on City property or other sites 
occupied by the Consultant while performing the duties and responsibilities of the 
Agreement. It is the responsibility of the Consultant to familiarize themselves with the 
requirements of this policy and to inform all their employees and sub-consultants of the 
requirements and ensure their compliance. If the Consultant, their employees or 
sub-consultants are found in violation of this policy, the Agreement may be terminated. 
 
7. 
INDEMNIFICATION/HOLD HARMLESS 
The Consultant agrees to indemnify, hold harmless, and defend the City from and against 
any and all liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures, and suits, and the cost and expenses 
incident thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, which may hereafter arise as a 
result the performance of the Consultant’s duties, including death or bodily injury to any 
person, destruction or damage to any property, contamination of or adverse effects on the 
environment, or any violation of governmental laws, regulations, or orders to the extent 
caused by (1) the Consultant’s breach of any term or provision of this Agreement; or (2) 
any negligent or wrongful act, error, or omission by the Consultant, or its employees or 
sub-consultants in the performance of this Agreement.  The Consultant acknowledges 
that it may incur a financial obligation to the City pursuant to the terms of this paragraph. 
 
8. 
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS 
The Consultant must provide proof of the following insurance coverages: 
 
 
Commercial General Liability Insurance 
For claims arising out of bodily injury, illness, or death, or from damage to or destruction 
of property of others, including loss or use thereof, with minimum limits of $1,000,000 
per occurrence, $2,000,000 aggregate for the entire term of the Agreement. 
 
 
Workers’ Compensation 
Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in effect for the entire term of the Agreement, 
as required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services under this 
Agreement.  Consultant shall provide the City with proof of workers’ compensation or 
employer’s liability insurance coverage. 
 
 
Professional Liability Insurance 
The Consultant shall provide proof of professional liability insurance or errors and 
omissions liability insurance in an amount not less than $500,000 to protect the City from 
any and all claims arising from the Consultant’s negligence in the performance of duties

under this Agreement.  The City prefers that this liability insurance coverage be 
provided pursuant to an “occurrence” policy.  
 
If this coverage is provided pursuant to a “claims made” policy: 
 
 
a.

provide the City with a certificate of insurance demonstrating that such

b. 
In the event the Consultant’s services extend into a future policy period,

c.

d.

of any payment request until the report is submitted. 
 
11. 
INDEPENDENT CONSULTANT 
The Consultant shall function as an independent consultant for the purposes of this

that may be incurred by the Consultant in fulfilling the terms of this Agreement. Nothing 
in this Agreement shall be interpreted as authorizing the Consultant or its agents or 
employees to act as an agent or representative of or on behalf of the City or to incur any 
obligation of any kind on the behalf of the City. 
 
12. 
TAXES 
The Consultant agrees to pay all valid taxes, excises, license fees, permit fees, bills, 
debts, and obligations incurred by and in connection with its operations under this 
Agreement. 
 
13. 
DEFAULT 
Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed to be a material 
element of the Agreement. In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform 
according to the terms of this Agreement, such party may be declared in default. 
 
14. 
REMEDIES 
In the event a party has been declared in default, such defaulting party shall be allowed a 
period of fifteen (15) days within which to cure the default.  In the event the default 
remains uncorrected, the non-defaulting party declaring default may elect to: 
 
 
a. 
Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not 
exceed the contract amount; or 
 
 
b. 
Treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance.

15. 
TERM AND TERMINATION 
The term of this Agreement shall be from date of execution to November 7, 2025.  The 
parties may by mutual written agreement renew or extend this Agreement. 
The City may, without cause, and upon thirty days’ written notice to the Consultant, 
terminate this Agreement in whole or in part at any time, for the City’s convenience. 
Upon receipt of such notice, the Consultant shall: 
 
 
a. 
Discontinue all services affected; and 
 
 
b.

reports, estimates, summaries, and such other information and materials as

Agreement, whether completed or in process. 
 
 
c.

ADDITIONAL REMEDIES 
In the event the Consultant fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement,

default of any of the conditions by either party which shall require the party not in default 
to commence legal or equitable action against the defaulting party each party shall bear 
its own costs and expenses, including without limitation, attorney’s fees.  
 
17. 
GOVERNING LAW, JURISDICTION AND VENUE 
The construction, interpretation, and enforcement of this Agreement shall be governed by 
the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of Wyoming shall have 
jurisdiction over any action arising out of this Agreement and over the parties, and the 
venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
18. 
GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNITY 
The City of Cheyenne, along with its officials and employees, does not waive its 
governmental immunity by entering into this Agreement, except to the extent necessary

Agreement.  Furthermore, the City specifically retains all immunities and defenses 
available to it as a sovereign or governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et

A. GENERAL CONDITIONS 
 
Standard of Care.  The standard of care for all professional engineering and related 
services performed or furnished by Engineer under this Agreement will be the care and 
skill ordinarily used by members of the subject profession practicing under similar

Technical Accuracy.  Owner shall not be responsible for discovering deficiencies in the

technical accuracy without additional compensation unless such corrective action is

Conflicts within the Agreement.  If there is a conflict within this Agreement, the order

(a) Agreement. 
(c) Any supplementary conditions to the General Conditions.

Any changes in the Consultant’s or Subconsultant’s staff or fee structure will be

Overhead and Normal Business Expenses.  The consultant shall include customary 
overhead costs and statutory benefits as part including, but not limited to, social security 
contributions, unemployment, excise and payroll taxes, workers’ compensation, health 
and retirement benefits, bonuses, sick leave, vacation, and holiday pay, as applicable, 
within the billing rates furnished in Schedule B.  Additionally, the cost of general and

including but not limited to, business taxes, legal, rent, utilities, office supplies, 
insurance, and other operating costs shall not be directly billed to project and assumed to 
be included within the billing rates furnished.
